For example, do you feel unable to stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yes, once I start, it's like a switch flips and I feel completely out of control; even when I tell myself to stop, my hands just keep going back for more. I also eat incredibly fast, barely chewing my food, almost like I'm in a race even though I'm completely alone.\n\nDoctor: During these eating episodes, do you feel very upset or guilty afterward, or worry a lot about your weight and body shape?\n\nPatient: I definitely feel a lot of shame and embarrassment about how much I'm putting away, which is why I lock myself in to do it. But as for feeling guilty afterward or worrying about my weight and body shape, I'm not really sure since those aren't things I've thought about much.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your self-worth is strongly tied to your weight or body shape, or that you are very distressed about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really thought about or been focused on my weight or body shape at all. My real distress comes from the deep loneliness after the divorce, the embarrassment of how I eat, and the physical pain in my stomach after a binge. My self-worth isn't tied to my appearance or weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods of restricting food, intense fear of gaining weight, or behaviors like purging, laxative use, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have an intense fear of gaining weight, and I've never purged, used laxatives, or exercised to control my weight. The only thing I do is sometimes skip my lunch break at work, but that's just so I can \"save up\" to binge at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat in secret because you feel ashamed or fear being judged, and do these episodes happen even when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'm so ashamed of how much I eat that I barely touch food around my family or soccer friends, choosing instead to lock myself in my apartment to binge in secret. I also eat when I'm not physically hungry at all, often going back to the kitchen for another huge plate or brownies even when I'm already completely full from dinner.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you intentionally restricted food because of your weight or shape, or used behaviors like fasting, purging, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I have never restricted my food because of my weight or shape. I have also never used purging, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to try to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your eating is mainly driven by a strong fear of gaining weight or a desire to change your body shape, or have you ever used vomiting, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to control your weight?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, my eating is not driven by a fear of gaining weight or a desire to change my body shape. I have also never used vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control my weight.

For example, do you feel unable to stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yes, once I start, it's like a switch flips and I feel completely out of control; even when I tell myself to stop, my hands just keep going back for more. I also eat incredibly fast, barely chewing my food, almost like I'm in a race even though I'm completely alone.\n\nDoctor: During these eating episodes, do you feel very upset or guilty afterward, or worry a lot about your weight and body shape?\n\nPatient: I definitely feel a lot of shame and embarrassment about how much I'm putting away, which is why I lock myself in to do it. But as for feeling guilty afterward or worrying about my weight and body shape, I'm not really sure since those aren't things I've thought about much.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your self-worth is strongly tied to your weight or body shape, or that you are very distressed about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really thought about or been focused on my weight or body shape at all. My real distress comes from the deep loneliness after the divorce, the embarrassment of how I eat, and the physical pain in my stomach after a binge. My self-worth isn't tied to my appearance or weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods of restricting food, intense fear of gaining weight, or behaviors like purging, laxative use, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have an intense fear of gaining weight, and I've never purged, used laxatives, or exercised to control my weight. The only thing I do is sometimes skip my lunch break at work, but that's just so I can \"save up\" to binge at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat in secret because you feel ashamed or fear being judged, and do these episodes happen even when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'm so ashamed of how much I eat that I barely touch food around my family or soccer friends, choosing instead to lock myself in my apartment to binge in secret. I also eat when I'm not physically hungry at all, often going back to the kitchen for another huge plate or brownies even when I'm already completely full from dinner.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you intentionally restricted food because of your weight or shape, or used behaviors like fasting, purging, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I have never restricted my food because of my weight or shape. I have also never used purging, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to try to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your eating is mainly driven by a strong fear of gaining weight or a desire to change your body shape, or have you ever used vomiting, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, my eating is not driven by a fear of gaining weight or a desire to change my body shape. I have also never used vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your self-worth depends on your weight or body shape, or that you are very distressed about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about my weight or body shape in that way, and my self-worth isn't tied to that at all. My distress is mainly about the out-of-control eating behavior itself and the deep embarrassment I feel over how much I consume when I'm alone."
  }

The only thing I do is sometimes skip my lunch break at work, but that's just so I can \"save up\" to binge at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat in secret because you feel ashamed or fear being judged, and do these episodes happen even when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'm so ashamed of how much I eat that I barely touch food around my family or soccer friends, choosing instead to lock myself in my apartment to binge in secret. I also eat when I'm not physically hungry at all, often going back to the kitchen for another huge plate or brownies even when I'm already completely full from dinner.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you intentionally restricted food because of your weight or shape, or used behaviors like fasting, purging, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I have never restricted my food because of my weight or shape. I have also never used purging, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to try to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your eating is mainly driven by a strong fear of gaining weight or a desire to change your body shape, or have you ever used vomiting, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, my eating is not driven by a fear of gaining weight or a desire to change my body shape. I have also never used vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your self-worth depends on your weight or body shape, or that you are very distressed about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about my weight or body shape in that way, and my self-worth isn't tied to that at all. My distress is mainly about the out-of-control eating behavior itself and the deep embarrassment I feel over how much I consume when I'm alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong fear of gaining weight or been preoccupied with your body shape or size because of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really thought about my weight or body image at all. My main struggle is more about the deep loneliness, the shame of eating this way, and feeling completely out of control once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods of significant weight loss, very low weight, or a diagnosis of another eating disorder related to these binge episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any periods of significant weight loss or had a very low weight. I've also never been diagnosed with any other eating disorder before."

My distress is mainly about the out-of-control eating behavior itself and the deep embarrassment I feel over how much I consume when I'm alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong fear of gaining weight or been preoccupied with your body shape or size because of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really thought about my weight or body image at all. My main struggle is more about the deep loneliness, the shame of eating this way, and feeling completely out of control once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods of significant weight loss, very low weight, or a diagnosis of another eating disorder related to these binge episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any periods of significant weight loss or had a very low weight. I've also never been diagnosed with any other eating disorder before.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes cause you significant distress on their own, even when you are not worried about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Yes, the episodes themselves cause me immense distress and shame, completely separate from any concerns about my weight. It is incredibly frustrating to feel that complete loss of control, like a switch flipping, and the deep embarrassment has led me to isolate myself from my friends and family. It's even affecting my job because I'm constantly tired and sluggish from going to bed so stuffed and uncomfortable."

I have also never used purging, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to try to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your eating is mainly driven by a strong fear of gaining weight or a desire to change your body shape, or have you ever used vomiting, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, my eating is not driven by a fear of gaining weight or a desire to change my body shape. I have also never used vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your self-worth depends on your weight or body shape, or that you are very distressed about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about my weight or body shape in that way, and my self-worth isn't tied to that at all. My distress is mainly about the out-of-control eating behavior itself and the deep embarrassment I feel over how much I consume when I'm alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong fear of gaining weight or been preoccupied with your body shape or size because of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really thought about my weight or body image at all. My main struggle is more about the deep loneliness, the shame of eating this way, and feeling completely out of control once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods of significant weight loss, very low weight, or a diagnosis of another eating disorder related to these binge episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any periods of significant weight loss or had a very low weight. I've also never been diagnosed with any other eating disorder before.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes cause you significant distress on their own, even when you are not worried about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Yes, the episodes themselves cause me immense distress and shame, completely separate from any concerns about my weight. It is incredibly frustrating to feel that complete loss of control, like a switch flipping, and the deep embarrassment has led me to isolate myself from my friends and family. It's even affecting my job because I'm constantly tired and sluggish from going to bed so stuffed and uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you usually eat much more quickly than normal, eat until you feel uncomfortably full, or eat large amounts when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do all of those. I eat incredibly fast, barely chewing and stuffing food in like I'm in a race, and I don't stop until my stomach is rock-hard and hurts to stand up. I also frequently keep eating—like grabbing another plate or starting on a pan of brownies—even when I'm already full and not hungry at all."
  }

My real distress comes from the deep loneliness after the divorce, the embarrassment of how I eat, and the physical pain in my stomach after a binge. My self-worth isn't tied to my appearance or weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods of restricting food, intense fear of gaining weight, or behaviors like purging, laxative use, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have an intense fear of gaining weight, and I've never purged, used laxatives, or exercised to control my weight. The only thing I do is sometimes skip my lunch break at work, but that's just so I can \"save up\" to binge at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat in secret because you feel ashamed or fear being judged, and do these episodes happen even when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'm so ashamed of how much I eat that I barely touch food around my family or soccer friends, choosing instead to lock myself in my apartment to binge in secret. I also eat when I'm not physically hungry at all, often going back to the kitchen for another huge plate or brownies even when I'm already completely full from dinner.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you intentionally restricted food because of your weight or shape, or used behaviors like fasting, purging, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I have never restricted my food because of my weight or shape. I have also never used purging, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to try to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your eating is mainly driven by a strong fear of gaining weight or a desire to change your body shape, or have you ever used vomiting, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, my eating is not driven by a fear of gaining weight or a desire to change my body shape. I have also never used vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your self-worth depends on your weight or body shape, or that you are very distressed about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about my weight or body shape in that way, and my self-worth isn't tied to that at all. My distress is mainly about the out-of-control eating behavior itself and the deep embarrassment I feel over how much I consume when I'm alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong fear of gaining weight or been preoccupied with your body shape or size because of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really thought about my weight or body image at all. My main struggle is more about the deep loneliness, the shame of eating this way, and feeling completely out of control once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods of significant weight loss, very low weight, or a diagnosis of another eating disorder related to these binge episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any periods of significant weight loss or had a very low weight. I've also never been diagnosed with any other eating disorder before.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes cause you significant distress on their own, even when you are not worried about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Yes, the episodes themselves cause me immense distress and shame, completely separate from any concerns about my weight. It is incredibly frustrating to feel that complete loss of control, like a switch flipping, and the deep embarrassment has led me to isolate myself from my friends and family. It's even affecting my job because I'm constantly tired and sluggish from going to bed so stuffed and uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you usually eat much more quickly than normal, eat until you feel uncomfortably full, or eat large amounts when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do all of those. I eat incredibly fast, barely chewing and stuffing food in like I'm in a race, and I don't stop until my stomach is rock-hard and hurts to stand up. I also frequently keep eating—like grabbing another plate or starting on a pan of brownies—even when I'm already full and not hungry at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever strongly fear gaining weight or try to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have a fear of gaining weight, and I've never used things like vomiting, laxatives, or extreme dieting to control it. The only thing I do is sometimes skip lunch at work, but that is just to \"save up\" room for my nighttime binges rather than to lose weight."
  }

    "content": "Candidate Diseases: \n[\"F50.8\", \"F50.9\"]\n\nInterview Transcript: \nDoctor: Hello, I’m Dr. ChatGPT. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hello, doctor. Since the divorce I eat huge meals by myself late at night because I’m embarrassed by how much I eat around other people. It’s been really hard for me to admit this to anyone.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating a lot, do you ever try to get rid of the food by vomiting, using laxatives, fasting, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do any of those things at all. I have never tried to throw up, use laxatives, or force myself to fast or over-exercise after I eat.\n\nDoctor: How often do these large eating episodes happen, and have they been going on for at least 3 months?\n\nPatient: They happen at least a few nights every week, usually when I get home late and cook those huge meals just for myself. It's been going on steadily like this for close to a year now, ever since my divorce was finalized.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you feel like you cannot control how much or how fast you are eating? For example, do you feel unable to stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yes, once I start, it's like a switch flips and I feel completely out of control; even when I tell myself to stop, my hands just keep going back for more. I also eat incredibly fast, barely chewing my food, almost like I'm in a race even though I'm completely alone.\n\nDoctor: During these eating episodes, do you feel very upset or guilty afterward, or worry a lot about your weight and body shape?\n\nPatient: I definitely feel a lot of shame and embarrassment about how much I'm putting away, which is why I lock myself in to do it. But as for feeling guilty afterward or worrying about my weight and body shape, I'm not really sure since those aren't things I've thought about much.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your self-worth is strongly tied to your weight or body shape, or that you are very distressed about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really thought about or been focused on my weight or body shape at all. My real distress comes from the deep loneliness after the divorce, the embarrassment of how I eat, and the physical pain in my stomach after a binge. My self-worth isn't tied to my appearance or weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods of restricting food, intense fear of gaining weight, or behaviors like purging, laxative use, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have an intense fear of gaining weight, and I've never purged, used laxatives, or exercised to control my weight. The only thing I do is sometimes skip my lunch break at work, but that's just so I can \"save up\" to binge at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat in secret because you feel ashamed or fear being judged, and do these episodes happen even when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'm so ashamed of how much I eat that I barely touch food around my family or soccer friends, choosing instead to lock myself in my apartment to binge in secret. I also eat when I'm not physically hungry at all, often going back to the kitchen for another huge plate or brownies even when I'm already completely full from dinner.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you intentionally restricted food because of your weight or shape, or used behaviors like fasting, purging, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I have never restricted my food because of my weight or shape. I have also never used purging, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to try to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your eating is mainly driven by a strong fear of gaining weight or a desire to change your body shape, or have you ever used vomiting, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, my eating is not driven by a fear of gaining weight or a desire to change my body shape. I have also never used vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your self-worth depends on your weight or body shape, or that you are very distressed about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about my weight or body shape in that way, and my self-worth isn't tied to that at all. My distress is mainly about the out-of-control eating behavior itself and the deep embarrassment I feel over how much I consume when I'm alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong fear of gaining weight or been preoccupied with your body shape or size because of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really thought about my weight or body image at all. My main struggle is more about the deep loneliness, the shame of eating this way, and feeling completely out of control once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods of significant weight loss, very low weight, or a diagnosis of another eating disorder related to these binge episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any periods of significant weight loss or had a very low weight. I've also never been diagnosed with any other eating disorder before.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes cause you significant distress on their own, even when you are not worried about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Yes, the episodes themselves cause me immense distress and shame, completely separate from any concerns about my weight. It is incredibly frustrating to feel that complete loss of control, like a switch flipping, and the deep embarrassment has led me to isolate myself from my friends and family. It's even affecting my job because I'm constantly tired and sluggish from going to bed so stuffed and uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you usually eat much more quickly than normal, eat until you feel uncomfortably full, or eat large amounts when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do all of those. I eat incredibly fast, barely chewing and stuffing food in like I'm in a race, and I don't stop until my stomach is rock-hard and hurts to stand up. I also frequently keep eating—like grabbing another plate or starting on a pan of brownies—even when I'm already full and not hungry at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever strongly fear gaining weight or try to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have a fear of gaining weight, and I've never used things like vomiting, laxatives, or extreme dieting to control it. The only thing I do is sometimes skip lunch at work, but that is just to \"save up\" room for my nighttime binges rather than to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you intentionally tried to lose weight or were very preoccupied with your body shape or size?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really focused on trying to lose weight or been preoccupied with my body shape or size. My main struggle has just been the uncontrollable eating itself and the deep shame surrounding it, rather than trying to diet."
  }

The only thing I do is sometimes skip lunch at work, but that is just to \"save up\" room for my nighttime binges rather than to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you intentionally tried to lose weight or were very preoccupied with your body shape or size?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really focused on trying to lose weight or been preoccupied with my body shape or size. My main struggle has just been the uncontrollable eating itself and the deep shame surrounding it, rather than trying to diet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told you have an eating disorder before, or have you ever had symptoms like eating non-food items, repeated vomiting, or other unusual eating behaviors?\n\nPatient: No, I have never been diagnosed with an eating disorder before. I have also never experienced symptoms like eating non-food items or repeated vomiting."

My self-worth isn't tied to my appearance or weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods of restricting food, intense fear of gaining weight, or behaviors like purging, laxative use, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have an intense fear of gaining weight, and I've never purged, used laxatives, or exercised to control my weight. The only thing I do is sometimes skip my lunch break at work, but that's just so I can \"save up\" to binge at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat in secret because you feel ashamed or fear being judged, and do these episodes happen even when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'm so ashamed of how much I eat that I barely touch food around my family or soccer friends, choosing instead to lock myself in my apartment to binge in secret. I also eat when I'm not physically hungry at all, often going back to the kitchen for another huge plate or brownies even when I'm already completely full from dinner.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you intentionally restricted food because of your weight or shape, or used behaviors like fasting, purging, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, I have never restricted my food because of my weight or shape. I have also never used purging, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to try to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your eating is mainly driven by a strong fear of gaining weight or a desire to change your body shape, or have you ever used vomiting, laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, my eating is not driven by a fear of gaining weight or a desire to change my body shape. I have also never used vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise to control my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your self-worth depends on your weight or body shape, or that you are very distressed about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about my weight or body shape in that way, and my self-worth isn't tied to that at all. My distress is mainly about the out-of-control eating behavior itself and the deep embarrassment I feel over how much I consume when I'm alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong fear of gaining weight or been preoccupied with your body shape or size because of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really thought about my weight or body image at all. My main struggle is more about the deep loneliness, the shame of eating this way, and feeling completely out of control once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods of significant weight loss, very low weight, or a diagnosis of another eating disorder related to these binge episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any periods of significant weight loss or had a very low weight. I've also never been diagnosed with any other eating disorder before.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes cause you significant distress on their own, even when you are not worried about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Yes, the episodes themselves cause me immense distress and shame, completely separate from any concerns about my weight. It is incredibly frustrating to feel that complete loss of control, like a switch flipping, and the deep embarrassment has led me to isolate myself from my friends and family. It's even affecting my job because I'm constantly tired and sluggish from going to bed so stuffed and uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you usually eat much more quickly than normal, eat until you feel uncomfortably full, or eat large amounts when you are not physically hungry?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do all of those. I eat incredibly fast, barely chewing and stuffing food in like I'm in a race, and I don't stop until my stomach is rock-hard and hurts to stand up. I also frequently keep eating—like grabbing another plate or starting on a pan of brownies—even when I'm already full and not hungry at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever strongly fear gaining weight or try to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have a fear of gaining weight, and I've never used things like vomiting, laxatives, or extreme dieting to control it. The only thing I do is sometimes skip lunch at work, but that is just to \"save up\" room for my nighttime binges rather than to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you intentionally tried to lose weight or were very preoccupied with your body shape or size?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really focused on trying to lose weight or been preoccupied with my body shape or size. My main struggle has just been the uncontrollable eating itself and the deep shame surrounding it, rather than trying to diet.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told you have an eating disorder before, or have you ever had symptoms like eating non-food items, repeated vomiting, or other unusual eating behaviors?\n\nPatient: No, I have never been diagnosed with an eating disorder before. I have also never experienced symptoms like eating non-food items or repeated vomiting.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong fear of gaining weight or felt that your self-worth depended on your body shape or size?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have a fear of gaining weight, and I don't feel like my self-worth is tied to my body shape or size. My eating is driven much more by the deep loneliness and emptiness I've felt since the divorce. It's just my way of trying to cope with being alone, rather than any concerns about my physical appearance."

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort. Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nNo, I haven't really thought about or been focused on my weight or body shape at all. Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
